    was a product defect. The harness's: the "legal" address was written as
    `https://n32-legal.example/hook`, which this policy refuses — an unresolvable name
    is a refusal — so the probe had booked a correct refusal as a pass until it needed
    the row to exist, and the count assertion cascaded from it. Switched to a literal
    public address, because a probe of an address policy must not depend on a resolver.

    The defect was the silent one the user's choice explicitly ruled out: with
    `ABET_ALLOW_PRIVATE_TARGETS=1`, `POST /api/webhooks` accepted
    `http://127.0.0.1:6379/` and said nothing, because the warning lived in
    `assertAllowedTarget` while the routes call `classifyTarget`. Every unit test and
    the static guard were green over it — the guard checks that a policy call sits above
    the fetch, and one did. The fix moves the log to `classifyTarget`'s own override
    returns, so it fires at whichever boundary honored the hatch; `egress.test.ts`
    captures `console.warn` to pin it; and the harness boots the tree a second time with
    the hatch set, which is the only way to prove the write boundary is not the silent
    half. 9/9 against the final bytes, live dev.db `8907ef1d426d` unchanged.

8.45.6 MUTANTS: WHICH ASSERTION EARNS WHICH REFUSAL

    All run in throwaway trees under `/srv/work` (`node_modules` hardlinked, source
    copied — never hardlinked, or writing the mutant writes the app). The committed
    files were restored and re-diffed after each.

      M1  leadgenius' send-site check deleted           -> egress-sites 2 fail, naming
          `src/lib/webhooks.ts:13  fetch(webhook.url)`. The guard is non-vacuous against
          real code, not only against synthetic source.
      M2  `169.254/16` deleted from the range table     -> egress.test 1 fail: "every
          address in the table classifies as measured".
      M3  the DNS catch returns ok:true                 -> egress.test 1 fail: "a public
          literal needs no resolver, and an unresolvable name is refused".
      M4  `console.warn` deleted from `allowOverride`   -> egress.test 1 fail (the hatch
          test) and verify-egress.py's eighth assertion. This is the round's real bug,
          and only the two halves together catch it: the unit test proves the log
          exists, the probe proves it exists at the boundary a caller actually hits.
      M5  `if (outcome.refused) return false;` deleted  -> abetapi retry.test 2 fail
          (`isRetryable`, `nextStatus`) — the two questions a refusal changes: retry or
          not, and park or back off.
      M6  `blankComments` back to the naive regexes     -> route-scanner 1 fail (the new
          URL test, reporting "erased the call that followed it on the line"), and
          against leadgenius' real source two `literal` sites become `UNPROTECTED`. The
          sibling new test (`callArgs` splitting on URL punctuation) stayed green under
          M6, which is what makes it a separate claim rather than a second copy of the
          first.
      M7  `importers_of`'s relative grep re-pointed at the path that never existed ->
          abetflow FAILS, 299/1. Proof the fix was load-bearing and that abetflow's
          import is real.
      M8  the leadworks non-use declaration deleted -> 299/1 ("imported by nothing");
          `leadworks/src/lib/webhooks.ts` created (then removed) -> 299/1 ("the premise
          of its non-use declaration no longer holds"). A declaration that expires is
          the difference between an exception and an excuse.

8.45.7 NUMBERS AFTER

    `route-scanner.test.ts` 13 -> 15 tests per app (the `blankComments` and `callArgs`
    URL-shape tests), `egress.test.ts` 6 and `egress-sites.test.ts` 2 in all six, and
    abetapi's `retry.test.ts` 16 -> 19. Family 938 -> 1001 (+36 +12 +12 +3): leadgenius
    206, leadworks 168, abetagent 148, abetflow 181, abetanalytics 135, abetapi 163.
    Gate `bash shared/check-all.sh` 18/18 in 79 s — suites 78/0, ui 6/0, auth drift
    300/0, guards drift 78/0 (was 48), queues 67/0, docs 100/0. Against the code: 27
    request sites, 7 now covered by a policy call, 0 uncovered.

    Commits, in the three steps they were made — the policy, the docstring counts, and
    the one figure the table itself got wrong: shared `a3c17c5`, `ebe05ca`, `96f471a`;
    leadgenius `2dbb38f`, `a5ac30f`, `c8c7f7b`; leadworks `e48cd88`, `93bf638`, `3472535`;
    abetagent `64d673f`, `8a486b8`, `86e674f`; abetflow `af8b554`, `a06d8fa`, `f4e0e42`;
    abetanalytics `b92283d`, `6783d86`, `f9bbecb`; abetapi `188902d`, `12928e5`,
    `837d449`. Gate 18/18 before each (84 s on the last); nothing pushed (#42 — the
    ledger itself is still outside version control, which is why these twenty-one hashes
    exist only in this file).

8.45.8 STILL OWED, RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED

    The check-then-fetch gap is real and stays open. `classifyTarget` resolves the name,
    judges every answer, and then hands the *name* to `fetch`, which resolves again; a
    resolver that answers publicly then privately wins. Closing it means connecting to
    the address that was checked, which no caller of `fetch` can do without a custom
    agent. The module says so in its own docstring rather than claiming the property.

    HTTP coverage is one app. Three write boundaries and one send site are pinned
    statically only — by `egress-sites.test.ts`, `egress.test.ts` and the byte-identity
    checks, not by a request. Widening it means giving the probe per-app credentials,
    which is #40's registry's job, not another assertion inside this harness.

    Egress is about *where*, not *what comes back*. abetagent puts the first
    `FETCH_MAX_CHARS` (4 000) characters of a fetched body into the agent's context;
    refusing `169.254.169.254` does not make a public page's text trustworthy input. That
    channel is the same family as #43's fail-closed `workspaceId` claim, and this round
    should not be read as closing it.

    leadworks still has webhook rows nothing sends (#17), and now also an egress module
    nothing imports, deliberately. abetflow's boundary remains send-only, which is right
    for the code and live-risk while #74/#75 stand: an anonymous public trigger
    supplies the run input that `renderTemplate` turns into an address. The `env`
    category is one hop deep, and the block window is 25 lines of text — both stated in
    the guard, both fail toward reporting a site as unexplained rather than safe.
    `ABET_ALLOW_PRIVATE_TARGETS` is process-wide, not per-request: a production process
    with it set has no egress policy, and what protects that is now the log, not a
    compile-time gate. `verify-egress.py` is not in the gate — §8.20.5's rule that a
    check needing servers goes unsipped.

    #32 — closed by this section.

8.46 REUNITE THE SIX validate.ts COPIES — ONE GRAMMAR, TWO ENVELOPES
     (2026-10-09, #28; shared/validation/engine.ts + shared/tests/validation-engine.test.ts
      + shared/{sync,check-}validation*.sh, all vendored; the six src/lib/validate.ts rewritten)

8.46.1 THE MEASURE THAT CAME FIRST

    The task said "six copies". Measured, it was not that simple, and the shape of
    the drift decided the whole approach, so the measure ran before any edit. Using
    the TypeScript compiler API to walk each `validate.ts` into its top-level
    declarations (`extract.cjs`, output cached in `matrix.json`; a hand-rolled brace
    scanner had truncated every declaration whose return annotation is an object
    literal and falsely reported `fail` and `readJson` as shared — caught only
    because `unprocessable` appearing in exactly one app contradicted it): 35 names
    total, 19 of them in all six files, and **13 of those 19 already byte-identical**
    once comments and whitespace are stripped — `JsonObject, JsonArray, Schema,
    ValueOf, Validated, Validation, DEFAULT_JSON_MAX_BYTES, DEFAULT_TEXT_MAX,
    ValidationProblem, NO_FIELD_TO_UPDATE, validateBody, short, jsonByteLength`.
    `validateBody` and the whole pre-`switch` half of `collect` — the part that
    decides what "absent", "null" and "required" mean — were never the problem.

    Six of the 19 differed, and they differed in two opposite directions.
    `Field`, `FieldKind`, `KindValue` and `collect` had drifted as *subsets*: each app
    carried the kinds and field options it happened to need and none of the others —
    per kind the number of distinct collector bodies across the six was text 3, int 2,
    number 2, bool 2, date 2, json 2, strings 2, enum 1. `describe` drifted the other
    way, in no subset relation to anything: three apps answer "an object", three still
    answer "a object" — and `describe`'s word *is* the client's answer ("must be text,
    got a object"), which is the bug the task title named. The partial surface
    (`bad` in 5, `fieldProblem` in 4, `boolFlag` in 4, `DEFAULT_LIST_MAX` in 3,
    `readJson` in 5, `parseStringList` in 3; `stringList`/`validateArray`/`asDate`/
    `malformed`/`unprocessable` each in one app) is the answer-shape, not the rule.

8.46.2 THE DECISION — AND WHY IT IS NOT JUST "MAKE THEM ONE FILE"

    Reunite the engine, consciously fork the envelope. The rules (no coercion,
    nothing dropped in silence, every problem named at once, absent ≠ null, text
    trimmed) are one thing and are now said once. The *answer* is not, and is
    deliberately not: `abetapi` publishes `{ error: { code, message }, fields }` at
    **422** because `src/lib/openapi.ts` publishes that envelope as its product —
    collapsing it into the family's `{ error: "…"; fields }` at 400 would break every
    client that reads `error.code`. So five apps keep 400, abetapi keeps 422, and the
    drift check pins that split as a *fork* rather than letting it rot into six apps
    each quietly picking its own status. One rules engine, two answers, both halves
    asserted. That is the reason this file exists apart from the four apps whose
    validation was already ported: the family shares the rules and this app keeps its
    contract.

8.46.3 WHAT SHIPPED

    `shared/validation/engine.ts` (471 lines), vendored to `src/lib/validation-engine.ts`
    in all six by `shared/sync-validation.sh`. Eight kinds — the union of every kind
    any app used — and every field option any app had invented (`itemMax`, `pattern`,
    `objectOnly`, `format: "email" | "url"`, `label`). One wording per refusal; where
    two copies worded the same rule differently the engine says it once, and each such
    choice is either a grammar fix or a rule one app already enforced and the others
    had not (§8.46.4). The genuinely new rule is the `default:` arm at the bottom of
    `collect`: a `switch` over `field.kind` with no `default` *silently drops* a field
    whose kind its own collector never implemented — no write, no refusal, a 201 for a
    request whose value went nowhere — and the six separate collectors each allowed
    exactly that. The engine refuses it.

    Each app's `validate.ts` shrank to its own half: 455→197 (leadgenius), 578→293
    (leadworks), 421→187 (abetagent), 472→221 (abetflow), 651→390 (abetanalytics),
    737→402 (abetapi). What survives there is the historical doc header, the engine
    imports, a re-export of the pure types so the ~181 route handlers and `fields.ts`
    compile untouched (zero changes to any handler), the local `Validation<S>` type,
    the thin `validateBody` wrapper (`collectBody` → `fail`), and each app's own
    `readJson`/`fail`/envelope helpers. Dead once `collect` moved — `parseStringList`
    in abetagent and abetflow — was deleted.

    `shared/tests/validation-engine.test.ts` (10 tests) is vendored into all six for
    the engine's own reason: `npm test` runs against each app's `node_modules`, so a
    suite reading one app's copy proves nothing about the other five. It asserts the
    sentence table at *full length* (a 12-case one-wrong-value-per-kind table), the
    article, the int ceiling, per-index list errors, blank/null/absent, and the
    `default` arm. Run from any app it checks that app's engine, so six green suites
    now *means* six apps that cannot answer a wrong value differently.

    `shared/check-validation-drift.sh` — 37 assertions per run: the engine and its
    test to byte identity, each `validate.ts` to still importing `./validation-engine`
    and having grown no `case "text"` of its own (an app that re-inlines `collect`
    leaves the vendored engine trusted by nothing — byte identity cannot see it, §8.20),
    each `validation-engine.test.ts` to still importing `../../src/lib/validation-engine`
    (not a re-typed local copy), the engine to not import `next/server` (the moment it
    does, it has started deciding the status the fork is about), and the envelope fork.
    Wired into the gate (`check-all.sh`, six shared checks → seven) and the console's
    `SMOKE_SUITES` (17 → 18 registered, 13 → 14 against `shared/`), with the two README
    counts and `ops-console/README.md`'s suite figures following (§8.45.4's lesson that a
    comment naming N is a claim, and `check-docs.sh` reads the file against the artifact).
    The six wording edits (#82) retarget the fragment matches that quoted the old
    sentences to the engine's: leadworks `tags`/`fields` now name the index, abetagent
    `tools` and abetflow `fields` likewise, abetapi `1 entries` → `one entry`, and
    abetanalytics' date line to the engine's `is not a date: "garbage"`.

8.46.4 WHAT BECAME STRICTER, RECORDED RATHER THAN BURIED IN A DIFF

    The union is not only a re-wording. Where one app enforced a rule four others had
    not, adopting the union makes those four stricter, and that is a behaviour change
    owed in prose: `int` now has a ceiling (`Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`) in every app
    that had none, so the `rateLimitPerMin: 1e21` that abetapi measured as a Prisma `Int`
    500 is now a refusal; `text` is length-checked before format, so a 5,000-character
    "URL" is refused for its length rather than echoed forty characters deep; `strings`
    is de-duplicated and trimmed engine-wide and errors are keyed per index, so abetapi's
    `scopes`/`events` now lose exact duplicates (harmless — every reader matches with
    `includes`) and a wrong element is named `tags[0]`, not a joined sentence; the
    `bool`/`date`/`json` sentences are unified to the engine's. The `describe` article is
    the pure fix the task started on: "a object" → "an object", six apps agreeing.
    The `default` arm changes no live behaviour today — no current table names an
    unimplemented kind — and is a tripwire for the future edit that would.

8.46.5 THE AFTER-PROBE — ONE BAD BODY, SIX ENGINES

    Two probes drive each app's *own* vendored code, not mocks, not `shared/`. The
    first feeds one bad body — `{ name: 42, rate: "1000", on: "no", urls: "a,b" }`,
    a wrong value in each of four kinds — to each app's `src/lib/validation-engine`.
    All six returned the identical combined refusal, character for character:

      name must be text, got a number  ||  rate must be a whole number, got "1000"
      as text — send it unquoted  ||  on must be true or false, got a string — send it
      unquoted  ||  urls must be an array of strings, got a string — send it as a
      one-element array

    The second calls each app's own `validateBody` and reads the `NextResponse` it
    builds — five report `status=400` with a `string` under `error` and sibling
    `fields`, abetapi reports `status=422` with `error.code: "invalid_request"` (its
    `fields` nested inside `error`, per its OpenAPI). One engine, two answers, both
    shown against shipped code. There is deliberately no six-server HTTP probe for
    this round: it changes internal factoring, not a route-observable answer, and the
    HTTP refusals are already pinned by §8.15/§8.17/§8.45 and each app's
    route-validation suites; a probe that needs six listening servers goes in the
    console, not the gate (§8.20.5). The engine-level probe is the family-agreement
    assertion the file exists for, and it runs the real engine in each app.

8.46.6 MUTANTS: WHICH ARM EARNS WHICH FAILURE

    Each edit was taken to a canonical file, synced, run, then restored from a backup;
    the restored tree re-ran to 37/0 and a 10/0 engine test.

      M1  abetagent's validate.ts grew a `case "text"`  -> drift 36/1, naming abetagent
          re-inlined the collector. The identity-invisible rot.
      M2  the engine's `default:` arm removed            -> drift STILL 37/0 (all six
          copies match the mutated canonical), but the engine test 9/1: the unimplemented
          kind is silently dropped. Identity necessary, not sufficient — the test is the
          behaviour.
      M3  `describe` reworded "an object"->"a object"    -> drift STILL green (byte
          identity to a wrong sentence is worthless), engine test 9/1. The full-length
          table, not a fragment match, is what earns this.
      M4  abetapi's envelope flipped 422 -> 400          -> drift 36/1, naming abetapi's
          422 half. The fork is a live assertion, not a comment.
      M5  the test's engine import retargeted, then synced so bytes still match -> drift
          fails on the test-import insistence in all six. Identical-and-unused is caught.

8.46.7 NUMBERS AFTER

    Family suites 1001 -> 1061 (+10 per app: the engine test in all six) — leadgenius
    216, leadworks 178, abetagent 158, abetflow 191, abetanalytics 145, abetapi 173,
    each including the 10 engine cases and each asserting the same sentence table.
    Gate `bash shared/check-all.sh` 18 -> 19 steps in 85 s — suites 83/0, ui 6/0,
    auth 300/0, guards 78/0, validation-drift 37/0, queues 67/0, docs 102/0 (the two new
    `shared/*.sh` now named in the README). `shared/` is twenty-one scripts: four `sync-*`,
    nine `check-*`, three `run-*-verify.sh`, five `verify-*.py`.

    Commits, one per repo, nothing pushed (#42 — the ledger is still outside version
    control, so these hashes exist only here): shared `a7db865`; leadgenius `884c38d`,
    leadworks `cb3728f`, abetagent `7fa0c07`, abetflow `a272f7e`, abetanalytics
    `5e9855e`, abetapi `fe09abd`.

8.46.8 STILL OWED, RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED

    #28's title — reunite-or-fork the six copies, starting with the `describe` fix — is
    closed. Two things it does not close are named rather than absorbed. First, §8.44's
    reader enhancement: `route-validation.test.ts` still answers "which table does this
    route declare", not "does this handler's write need a table it never builds"; a
    handler that writes without consulting the engine still passes. The engine made the
    *rules* one file; making the *coverage* provable per route is the next question.
    Second, this round reunified wording and did not audit the wording's *correctness*
    against every caller — an app that had a friendlier sentence than the union's
    inherited the union's, which is the point of one voice but is a change a client that
    string-matched the old message would notice. That risk is why the sentence table is
    asserted at full length rather than by fragment: the change is visible in one place.

8.47 THE PORT EXTRACTOR THAT ONLY RAN WITH THE STACK UP
     (2026-10-09, #47; shared/check-live.sh)

    `check-live.sh` deliberately lives outside the playbook gate — a check that needs
    six listening servers is a check that goes unsipped (§8.20.5). That decision is
    sound and stays, but it left the suite's own load-bearing half, the regex that
    decides *which* ports to probe, with no assertion any machine without servers
    could run. And it was wrong in two ways measured against the real README: it
    grepped `localhost:[0-9]{4}` only, so the docs' `127.0.0.1:3800` never entered the
    list; and it matched one `:?[0-9]{4}` per table row, so `| AgentWork SDK | 8000 /
    3103 |` yielded 8000 and dropped 3103. Nine ports probed of eleven promised — the
    two missing ones both already had a documented path in the `case` a few lines
    below, which is how the skip was so easy to miss: the tool clearly intended to
    check them.

    Fixed by pulling the extraction into `extract_ports` (both host spellings; every
    `\b[0-9]{4}\b` on a `^\|` row, safe because the only 4-digit runs in the tables are
    ports — the price column stays under four digits) and pinning it with a self-test
    that needs no server and runs the *same function the suite uses*, not a copy: the
    fixture carries a `127.0.0.1:` mention and a two-port cell, and the extractor must
    return all four ports. The mutant is the pre-fix extractor run against the same
    fixture — it returns only `{3700, 8000}`, so the guard is non-vacuous against both
    the host-form miss and the multi-port miss. Extraction against the whole README now
    yields all 11 (3101–3103, 3200–3700, 3800, 8000). Shared `3bde922`; check-suites
    83/0, check-docs 102/0 still green (this suite is not in the gate, so the fix cannot
    regress it and needed no gate re-run).

    The lesson generalises past this file: a check excluded from the gate must still
    carry a server-free assertion of its *own selection logic*, or the thing that would
    catch it skipping work is the work itself.

8.48 ABETAPI'S diagnose-webhook DECIDES ITS CREDENTIAL IN THE ONE MODULE (#48)
     (2026-10-09, abetapi/src/app/api/ai/diagnose-webhook/route.ts; shared/check-auth-drift.sh, shared/README.md, shared/verify-ai-claim.py)

8.48.1 THE MEASUREMENT THE TASK DEMANDED FIRST

    #48 carried a hard precondition: "abetapi answers 401 through requireKey, not through
    each app's withAuth/withAiAuth pair, so its error envelope has never been compared to
    the canonical `{ error: message }`. Measure the current 401/404 shapes over HTTP on a
    copy of its dev.db before moving it." `shared/verify-ai-claim.py` is the ninth harness
    and does exactly that, reusing `verify-cross-tenant.py`'s sandbox-tree, scratch-copy
    and live-checksum primitives rather than re-deriving them. The BEFORE run against the
    route as it stood:

      C  victim key + own webhookId     -> 200  {diagnosed:false, stats{total:0…}}
      A  no credential                  -> 401  {error:{code:"authentication_error", message:…}}
      B  victim key + unknown webhookId -> 404  {error:"webhook not found"}
      D  victim key + FOREIGN workspaceId (text)  -> 200   (the claim was dropped)
      E  victim key + NON-TEXT workspaceId (42)   -> 200   (the claim was dropped)
      F  victim key + EMPTY workspaceId           -> 200   (correct — "" is absent)
      G  malformed JSON bytes           -> 400  {error:{code:"invalid_request", message:…}}

    Two facts came out that the task title did not state. abetapi's *auth* refusals are an
    object envelope (`{error:{code,message}}`) and its *row* refusals a bare string
    (`{error:"webhook not found"}`) — two shapes in one route, because the first is
    `requireKey`'s published-API answer and the second is what `ai-panel.tsx` prints as
    `data.error`. And the route was safe but not unified: `DIAGNOSE_POST` names no
    `workspaceId`, and `collect` iterates the schema, so a body claim of any type was
    silently dropped — D and E answered 200 by diagnosing the *credential's* webhook. The
    lookup is scoped to the credential, so nothing cross-tenant ever read; the defect is
    that this was the one AI route that could not be steered by a body field at all, the
    opposite failure to the three the module was written to unify (#41's siblings refuse a
    foreign claim; this one ignored it). Both are the same disagreement #43 closed
    everywhere else, and "safe because ignored" is not the rule.

8.48.2 WHAT SHIPPED

    The route now resolves through `resolveAiActor`, the same call the three sibling AI
    routes make, and reads the claim off the *unvalidated* parsed body — `""` stays absent,
    foreign text is refused, a non-text claim is refused for naming no workspace. The body
    is read before the credential only to hand the claim over; `validateBody` still runs
    after `resolveAiActor`, so the documented property — a field-level answer requires a
    credential, which is why `{}` with no key used to answer 400 "webhookId required" — is
    preserved, and an unparseable body is refused before anyone is identified in exactly
    the shape the siblings already answer.

    The one seam is `requireKey`'s model (it *returns* a refusal response, already logged,
    already carrying `code` and rate-limit headers) against the module's (it *throws*). A
    route-local `KeyRefusal extends Error` carries `requireKey`'s response across that seam
    untouched and re-raised as-is, so `requireKey`'s envelope and its internal
    `logRequest` stay `requireKey`'s; the module's own `AiAuthError` is caught and rendered
    bare `{error: message}` — byte-identical to what `withAiAuth` returns in the other
    three apps, and the shape this route already keeps for its dashboard-visible refusals.
    Refusals of an authenticated call go through the route's existing `refused()` wrapper,
    which logs on the key path and stays silent on a browser session (no key row), the same
    asymmetry the 404 already had.

    The AFTER run: A, B, C, F, G byte-for-byte unchanged; D 200 -> 403
    `{error:"workspaceId does not belong to this API key."}`; E 200 -> 400
    `{error:"workspaceId must be text."}` — the module's two refusals, matching the
    siblings. And the two the probe added for #33's lesson, that static reading of
    `refused()` cannot settle: H the foreign refusal reached `RequestLog` (a POST row at
    403 on this path, count grew), I the non-text refusal reached it at 400. live dev.db
    unchanged.

8.48.3 THE GUARD THAT ADOPTS THE DECISION ALSO RETIRES ITS EXCUSE

    `check-auth-drift.sh` carried `abetapi|ai-actor.ts|present|src/app/api/ai/diagnose-webhook/route.ts`
    — a declaration that abetapi vendors the module *deliberately unused*, with the route
    file's existence as the premise that made it not-dead. That excuse existed precisely
    because the route did not import it. `resolveAiActor` gives the module a real importer,
    and the check's own rule ("declared non-use but has N importers — the declaration is
    stale") then fails it. So #48's edit is incomplete without deleting the declaration:
    a file that is used needs no premise. The drift check now *requires* abetapi to import
    `ai-actor.ts` — revert the route and `importers_of` reads zero, "vendored and imported
    by nothing," which is the positive form of the guard. Mutant: strip the `@/lib/ai-actor`
    import from the route (backup, edit, restore, never through git) -> 299/1 naming that
    file; restore -> 300/0.

8.48.4 NUMBERS AFTER, AND WHAT STAYS OPEN

    Gate `bash shared/check-all.sh` 19/19 in 80 s: suites 909 -> unchanged, abetapi 173/173;
    auth drift 300/0, docs 103/0 (+1 — the new harness is named in `shared/README.md`, whose
    count went twenty-one -> twenty-two scripts, eight -> nine harnesses, five -> six
    `verify-*.py`). `verify-ai-claim.py` is not in the gate — like `verify-egress.py` it
    starts a server, and a check that needs servers goes unsipped (§8.20.5); the Operations
    Console runs it as a suite.

    Only abetapi's diagnose route moved. The rest of abetapi's surfaces, and the other five
    apps' non-AI routes, still resolve session-or-key in their own `authedWorkspaceId` —
    that is #43's apikeys.ts half, and 59 call sites measured before this, not this one
    route. The check-then-fetch egress gap (§8.45.8) and the model-as-trusted-input channel
    (#43's fail-closed workspaceId claim, which reads a body field into context) are
    untouched: this made one route refuse a *wrong* workspace, not one route distrust what
    a *correct* workspace asks it to fetch.

    #48 — closed by this section.

8.49 ONE MODULE IS NOT DEAD JUST BECAUSE THE ROUTER DOES NOT REACH IT (#45)
     (2026-10-09, shared/sync-auth.sh + shared/check-auth-drift.sh + abetagent/src/components/ai-panel.tsx)

8.49.1 THE TASK SAID "DECIDE", AND THE MEASUREMENT SAID THE TWO MODULES WERE NOT THE SAME

    #45 asked whether abetagent should vendor `ai-actor.ts` and `ai-panel.tsx` at all.
    `check-auth-drift.sh` was already carrying both as `NON_USE` exceptions on the premise
    `absent|src/app/api/ai` — vendored, imported by nothing, excused because abetagent has
    no AI route. The obvious move was to stop vendoring both and retire the two exceptions.
    Running the change proved that move wrong, and it was wrong in the specific way this
    campaign keeps meeting: the file the runtime does not reach is not necessarily the file
    nothing reaches.

    `abetagent` has `tests/unit/ai-claim.test.ts`, vendored by `sync-guards.sh`, whose very
    first line imports `../../src/lib/ai-actor` to exercise `classifyWorkspaceClaim`
    offline — the coverage the ai-actor docstring promises "in all six apps." Deleting
    `ai-actor.ts` from the app broke abetagent's typecheck (`TS2307: Cannot find module
    '../../src/lib/ai-actor'`). The resolver is load-bearing for the suite even though no
    `src/**` route calls it, and it only looked dead because `importers_of` greps `src/`,
    not `tests/`. `ai-panel.tsx`, the browser component, is genuinely reached by nothing —
    no page renders it and a `.ts` test does not import a `.tsx`. One module stays, one
    goes; the uniform "drop both" would have quietly dropped real coverage.

8.49.2 WHAT SHIPPED: AN EXCLUSION IS A NON-USE POINTED THE OTHER WAY

    A per-app `EXCLUDE` map was added to `sync-auth.sh` and `check-auth-drift.sh`, keyed
    `app|name` and valued by a witness path. `ai-panel.tsx` is excluded from `abetagent`
    while `src/app/api/ai` is absent. sync-auth honours the same premise the check does (so
    they cannot diverge) and `rm -f`'s any copy an earlier uniform sync left behind — the
    deletion happens on the next sync, not by hand. The byte loop, which would otherwise
    fail abetagent for "has no ai-panel.tsx", instead insists the copy be *absent*: an
    excluded file that reappears is a FAIL, which is the whole point of not shipping it.
    A second loop flags the stale direction: grow `src/app/api/ai` and the exclusion fails
    with "the excluded AI panel must be vendored and imported," forcing the copy back on.
    The importer loop skips excluded pairs (there is no copy to be reached).

    `ai-actor.ts` goes back to `NON_USE` with the reason corrected — not "the app has no AI
    surface" but "reached only by the offline claim test, which `importers_of` cannot see."
    Its premise still expires on `src/app/api/ai`, because the day abetagent grows an AI
    route it gains a real source importer and needs no exception.

8.49.3 THE MUTANTS, IN BOTH DIRECTIONS

    M1  re-add the dead `ai-panel.tsx` copy           -> check-auth-drift 299/1, naming
        "abetagent vendors … but it is excluded here (#45)." The exclusion is not just "don't
        sync it," it is "fail if it comes back."
    M2  create `abetagent/src/app/api/ai` (empty)     -> check-auth-drift 4 fail: the stale
        "exclusion is stale" arm plus the byte loop now requiring the copy ("has no
        ai-panel.tsx") and the importer loop reaching it. Removing the dir restores 300/0.
    (Both ran against the working tree with files copied back and dirs removed; the tree
    afterwards shows only `D src/components/ai-panel.tsx` — `abetagent/src/app` was never
    disturbed, because `rmdir` on a non-empty directory fails and the mutant dirs were only
    the ones the run created.)

8.49.4 NUMBERS AFTER

    abetagent typecheck clean, suite 158/158 (unchanged — the claim test still runs against
    the retained resolver). `check-auth-drift` 300/0; the gate `bash shared/check-all.sh`
    19/19 in 80 s. `shared/README.md`'s non-use table went three rows to two-plus-one
    (abetapi's ai-actor row left with #48; the leadworks/egress row kept; abetagent now one
    retained-vendor and one exclusion), and its "20 canonical files / 120 assertions / 288
    total" prose was corrected to the real 21 modules and 300 checks it had been
    under-counting since `egress.ts` was added — a stale number in the file whose job is to
    describe the count.

8.49.5 WHAT THIS DOESN'T CLOSE

    abetagent still vendors `ai-actor.ts` with no runtime caller. That is now an accurate
    statement ("the test reaches it"), not a hand-wave; but if the offline claim test were
    ever narrowed to the apps that actually route through the resolver, abetagent would
    become excludable too — a decision #45 records rather than makes. The broader `#43` half
    (59 `authedWorkspaceId` call sites deciding claims in five un-vendored copies) is
    untouched; `importers_of` still cannot see a `tests/` importer, which is precisely the
    blind spot that made `ai-actor.ts` look dead and which #49's exclusion had to reason
    around rather than fix.

    #45 — closed by this section.

8.50 ONE THREAD PER SUITE IS A REGISTRY-SIZED POOL, AND THE REGISTRY ONLY GROWS (#49)
     (2026-10-09, ops-console/serve.py + shared/check-suites.sh)

8.50.1 THE THREAD POOL WAS THE EASY HALF; THE MEMORY WAS THE FINDING

    `ops-console/serve.py`'s `run_tests()` had `max_workers=len(SMOKE_SUITES)`. The
    suite count went 13 → 18 since the last measurement, and every addition has widened
    the pool by one automatically. On a box that reports 2 CPUs and, with six dev
    servers plus a Docker daemon already resident, ~2.2 GB `MemAvailable` with ~2.4 GB
    already in swap, "pool tracks the registry" is a slow-motion bug. But #49's claim
    needed measuring before the fix, and the measurement said the pool was misleading
    about what it oversubscribes.

    A process-tree RSS sampler (`/proc/*/stat` walked on every 0.2 s tick; peak summed
    across descendants) run against each class of suite in isolation:
      - the seven drift / docs / queues / suites gates in `shared/`: 3–19 MB each. curl
        and grep. Nothing to see.
      - `verify-auth.py`, `verify-ai-ui.py`, `verify-builder.py`: 8 MB each. They curl
        an already-running dev server; they do not spawn one.
      - `leadgenius/scripts/smoke.sh`: 240 MB against a live server.
      - `run-retry-verify.sh`, `run-teams-verify.sh`: 333 MB and 330 MB. Both go through
        `npx prisma db push` then `npx tsx scripts/verify-*.mts`, and tsx bootstraps a
        fresh V8 heap that loads the app's queue module and engine in-process. That
        ~330 MB is the number that matters, and `run-token-verify.sh` does the same six
        times sequentially (one tsx at a time within its own thread, not six).

    So the risk was never "18 next-dev servers booting at once". It was the three
    in-process `npx tsx` suites — token, retry, teams — overlapping and each carrying
    ~330 MB of new V8 heap alongside the already-resident servers and swap. 3×330 ≈ 1 GB
    of heap at t=0, on a box that had ~2.2 GB free and one dev server per app already
    using some of it. And a future suite that also booted something would push the peak
    up without anyone touching the pool.

8.50.2 THE FIX: A NAMED CONSTANT WITH THE MEASURED BASIS WRITTEN INTO THE FILE

    `MAX_CONCURRENT_SUITES = 4`, one module-level constant, with the measured numbers
    and the reasoning in a comment above it. Chosen so that the three heavy tsx suites
    still overlap (the run cannot be slower than its heaviest suite plus the ~5 s curl
    gates) while worst-case new heap stays ≈ 1 GB — under half of the free RAM — and the
    pool stops growing with the registry. Four is a bound on concurrency, not a
    correctness rule. It leaves headroom on this specific box: any fourth heavy suite
    that comes along fits within the same budget rather than silently opening a fifth
    slot in the pool.

    `run_tests()` now does `with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=MAX_CONCURRENT_SUITES)`.
    `api_overview()` still uses `len(SERVICES)`: its "workers" are HTTP GETs against
    already-running servers, and the count is a different registry (`SERVICES`, not
    `SMOKE_SUITES`). Out of scope.

8.50.3 A CAP WITH NO CHECK THAT READS IT IS JUST A COMMENT

    The family rule is that a fix has to survive the next well-meaning addition. So
    `check-suites.sh`, which already reads `SMOKE_SUITES` out of the AST, grew a section
    5 that reads `run_tests`' `ThreadPoolExecutor` call and its `max_workers` keyword,
    resolves the keyword against the module-level integer constants, and fails if:
      - `max_workers=len(SMOKE_SUITES)` (the mutant this closes — "spawns one thread per
        registered suite"),
      - `max_workers` names no module-level integer (a bare literal, a value computed at
        run time, a `min(...)`) — the constant has to be readable for the check to bind;
      - the resolved value is < 1 (nothing runs) or >= `len(SMOKE_SUITES)` (does not
        bind — every heavy suite still starts at t=0).
    All four directions verified against the working tree.

    A first cut of the assertion used `IFS=$'\t' read -r a b c` to split the AST's
    output line, and `read` collapsed an empty middle field — the bare-literal mutant
    `max_workers=2` silently passed, because `cap_val` picked up "18" and `n_suites`
    picked up "" and the whole chain read green. Measured and fixed: the sentinel is
    now the string `NONE` emitted in place of the empty field, so the field never
    collapses and the branch fires. The lesson generalises: an AST→bash fact transfer
    that uses empty fields as absence is not a transfer at all.

8.50.4 NUMBERS AFTER

    Green gate: `bash shared/check-all.sh` 19/19 in 81 s. `check-suites` 84/0 (was
    83/0 — section 5 adds one pass). All six app typechecks clean and all six suites at
    their existing counts (216/178/158/191/145/173). `serve.py`'s `run_tests` now
    imports under `max_workers=MAX_CONCURRENT_SUITES`, verified by `inspect.getsource`.
    No push, no commit of serve.py — ops-console is gitignored by #42 and cannot be
    committed. The guard (`check-suites.sh`) lives in the shared repo, so it does get
    committed alongside the ledger.

8.50.5 WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE

    The cap is per-suite-thread, not per-node-process. If a future suite runs several
    tsx invocations *concurrently* within itself, that suite's own footprint is not
    bounded by 4 threads. The 330 MB figure is also measured in isolation; the actual
    peak of an overlap depends on which curl suites are still in flight. A more refined
    fix would give each suite a weight and cap total weight, but that needs a registry
    column and a scheduling discipline; 4 was chosen because the three heavy suites are
    the same class and the light ones cost nothing. And #49's own title said "17 now";
    the registry is at 18 today — the thread count had already drifted once since the
    task was written, which is exactly the argument for capping the pool on a constant
    rather than on the registry.

    #49 — closed by this section.

8.51 A `-n 13` ON A FLUSHALL LOOKS LIKE ISOLATION AND IS NOT (#63)
     (2026-10-09, shared/check-queues.sh + shared/README.md)

8.51.1 THE COMMAND LOOKED SCOPED; FLUSHALL IGNORES THE SCOPE

    Filed from §8.32.7 — round #57's own cleanup. To clear its scratch db 13 it ran
    `redis-cli -p 6379 -n 13 flushall`. The `-n 13` reads as "act on db 13." FLUSHALL is
    server-wide: `-n` selects a db for the connection and FLUSHALL then empties every
    database, live db 0 included. Blast radius measured at the time: no business data
    (products persist to SQLite, not Redis — six dev.db shas identical before/after,
    leadgenius `26478a71…` unchanged), no in-flight jobs (abetagent's `bull:runs`
    wait/delayed/active/paused/completed/failed all length 0, and the worker re-minted
    `bull:runs:meta` within seconds). What died was bullmq `:meta` marker hashes for
    queues that had never held a job — rewritten by the Queue constructor on the next
    `new Queue()` touch, i.e. any boot or /api/health poll. No harm, but the safety was
    an accident of this portfolio's storage choice, not a property of the command.

    The dev.db discipline (§8.12) says "run against a COPY." Redis has no copy-to-point-
    at — the same-named db is the live keyspace — so the analogue cannot be "scope the
    db." It has to be a rule about verbs.

8.51.2 WHAT SHIPPED: RULE 12 IN check-queues.sh

    Added as rule 12 rather than a new check file — a new `shared/check-*.sh` would have
    to join check-all.sh's fixed `for c in …` list and ripple the README's script counts
    (§8.49.4's stale-number trap); check-queues.sh is already Redis-aware and already
    gated, and #63 explicitly allows "a new or extended shared/check-*.sh." It scans the
    harness scripts that reach Redis (`shared/run-*-verify.sh`, `shared/verify-*.py`,
    each app's `scripts/verify-*.mts` and its `tests/unit/queue.test.ts`) and asserts:
      (a) no server-wide verb in any `redis-cli` command — FLUSHALL/SWAPDB/SLAVEOF/
          REPLICAOF/`SCRIPT FLUSH`/SHUTDOWN — refused even with a `-n`;
      (b) a db-local `redis-cli` write (FLUSHDB, DEL, …) must name its db (`-n`/`--db`),
          since a bare one writes db 0;
      (b') programmatically, `.flushAll()` is refused unconditionally (ioredis sends
          FLUSHALL, which ignores the connected db like the CLI's), while `.flushdb()` is
          allowed only with a `/N` connection string or `.select(N)` in the file.
    The scan is narrow on purpose — it reads only `redis-cli` command lines and
    `.flushAll(`/`.flushdb(` calls — so `redis-cli MONITOR`/`--scan` in prose and the
    legitimate `shutdownWorkers` drain do not trip it.

8.51.3 THE TWO BUGS THE FIRST CUT HAD (both caught by running mutants)

    M-regex: `(a) first used `redis-cli[^\n]*flushall`. In a bracket, `[^\n]` is the set
    "not backslash and not the letter n," so it stops dead on the `n` of `-n 13` — the
    exact token in #63's command — and the FLUSHALL mutant PASSED. Fixed to `redis-cli.*`
    (grep is line-based, so `.` never crosses a newline anyway). This is the same class as
    #49's empty-field mutant: a regex/class that silently fails to match the thing it was
    written for, invisible until you run the mutant.

    M-scope: the first `.flushAll`/`.flushdb` branch excused either when the file had a
    `/13` string. Wrong for FLUSHALL — scoping a client does not make FLUSHALL
    db-local. Split into "flushAll always refused" and "flushdb refused unless scoped,"
    and the mutant `new IORedis("…:6379/13"); r.flushAll()` now flips it red.

8.51.4 NUMBERS AFTER

    `check-queues.sh` 67 -> 71 passed (rule 12's four ok arms: server-wide-none,
    unscoped-write-none, flushAll-none, flushdb-none), 0 failed. Full gate
    `bash shared/check-all.sh` 19/19 in 77 s; `check-docs` still 103/0 over the new README
    section. The harness file mutated during the probe (`run-token-verify.sh`) is restored
    byte-identical to HEAD (empty `git diff`). `shared/README.md` gained "A scratch Redis
    db is not a firewall" and a pointer to it in the check-queues rule list.

8.51.5 WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE

    #63 also asked whether a probe may FLUSHDB db 13 at all when other probes share it —
    that is left as stated discipline (prefer DEL of your own key prefix) rather than a
    hard rule, because db-13-sharing is not currently detectable from source: two probes
    naming `REDIS_URL=…/13` in separate files is legal and the check cannot know one will
    FLUSHDB while the other is mid-run. The rule guards the server-wide verbs and the
    db-0 default; the "don't clobber a neighbour's db 13" half stays with the harness
    author. And this guards SCRIPTS, not an interactive `redis-cli` a human or agent
    types at a shell — the original #63 was exactly that — so the README text is the part
    that reaches a person about to type FLUSHALL.

    #63 — closed by this section.

8.52 OPS-CONSOLE AND THE LEDGER FINALLY HAVE HISTORY (#42)
     (2026-10-09, ops-console/.git* + docs/ + shared/check-docs.sh)

8.52.1 THE DEFECT WAS THE ABSENCE OF A COMMIT, NOT THE PRESENCE OF A FILE

    /srv/work/.gitignore:10 ignores /products/, and each product carries its own
    nested .git so the ignore is not a bug — it is the layout. Two things sat
    under /products/ with no repo of their own: ops-console/ (serve.py, which
    decides which smoke suites run and, since #49, bounds them with
    MAX_CONCURRENT_SUITES; README.md; static/index.html) and — worse, because it
    is not under /products/ at all — the ledger in /root, where `git rev-parse`
    fails outright. The #49 edit to serve.py was real, load-bearing and
    unversionable: the round that added a measured concurrency cap could not
    commit the cap. #50's and #63's ledger appends were the portfolio's
    institutional memory with no memory of their own. Measured before acting:
    ops-console had no .git (`git rev-parse` resolved to the parent /srv/work
    work-tree, not a nested repo), a secrets/env scan (`find` for .env, credentials,
    *.key, id_rsa) was clean, history.json was the only runtime artifact, and
    sibling conventions were noted (shared=master, leadgenius/abetapi=main;
    parent=/srv/work=master). No git config was read or written; branch names came
    from per-command `-c` flags and `git init -b`, never from `git config`.

8.52.2 THE FIX MIRRORED THE FAMILY LAYOUT, NOT A NEW ONE

    `git init -b master` inside ops-console (master to match parent and shared, the
    two this repo is read alongside), a purpose-built .gitignore covering the
    artifacts serve.py regenerates — `history.json` (HISTORY = ROOT/"history.json",
    the last 40 runs save_history writes), `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc`. Staged the
    four source files by name (never -A) and committed. serve.py was then
    committable for the first time, and #49's cap arrived in history as a
    side-effect of the repo that makes it reviewable.

    The ledger went to a dedicated `docs/` repo — the task's first sanctioned
    option — over "inside ops-console", because the ledger documents the whole
    family (six apps plus shared/ and ops-console/) and grows one appended section
    per round; tying its history to the console's deploys would be wrong. The file
    was `mv`'d to docs/abetworks-improvements.txt and `/root/abetworks-improvements.txt`
    replaced by a symlink to it, so the append path every prior round used keeps
    working and there is no second copy to drift. Reversible: a move plus a symlink,
    nothing deleted. docs/ ships README.md (why the repo exists, what the symlink
    is) and the ledger; it produces no runtime state, so it needs no .gitignore.

8.52.3 THE GUARD ASSERTS PROVENANCE, BECAUSE NOTHING ELSE DOES

    Every other check reads files, not git state: `rm -rf ops-console/.git` would
    restore #42's defect and leave all 107 prior check-docs arms green. So a new
    check-docs section asserts the load-bearing files are *tracked*, not merely
    present — `git ls-files --error-unmatch serve.py` is exactly the question "is
    this file in this repo's index," true only if the repo and the commit both
    exist. Five arms: ops-console/.git present, serve.py tracked, docs/.git
    present, the ledger tracked, history.json ignored (not absent).

    All five non-vacuous, proven on a copy of the tree (`cp -a`, so .git came
    along) with one mutation applied at a time: dropping either .git flips its
    presence arm; and the decisive ones — `git rm --cached serve.py` flips ONLY
    arm 2 while arms 1/5 stay green, and `git rm --cached` on the ledger flips
    ONLY arm 4 — because the failure #42 was is precisely "file present, repo
    present, no history." Un-ignore by emptying ops-console/.gitignore flips arm 5
    alone. check-docs 103 -> 108.

8.52.4 NUMBERS AFTER

    Full gate `bash shared/check-all.sh` 19/19 in 71 s; check-docs 108/0. Two new
    repos, each with exactly one commit (ops-console aedc5db, docs ce51421);
    shared's check-docs.sh edit commits with this section. The parent /srv/work
    repo still reports both new files as ignored by .gitignore:10 — verified with
    `git check-ignore -v` — so the deliberate /products/ layout is untouched.

8.52.5 WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE

    The guard pins ops-console and the ledger specifically; it does not generalise
    to "every directory under /products/ that should be a repo is one" — abetConnect
    and abetworks-agentwork are not vendored apps and are out of the gate by design,
    and nothing here forces a new sibling to init a repo. And the `/root` symlink is
    machine-local: on another host the ledger is read from docs/ directly, so a round
    that assumes the /root path exists (the heredoc-appends here do) needs the symlink
    recreated — the real file is docs/abetworks-improvements.txt, and that is the path
    a fresh checkout should treat as canonical.

    #42 — closed by this section.

8.53 THE RETENTION DEFAULT IS GUARDED AT THE GATE, NOT JUST IN FIVE TESTS (#71 guard half)
     (2026-10-09, shared/check-queues.sh + shared/README.md)

8.53.1 THE CONVENTION HAD A GUARD THAT COULD NOT SEE A DROPPED OPTION

    #61 found leadgenius keeping every completed job; #64 fixed the family by putting
    `JOB_RETENTION` on every construction as `defaultJobOptions: JOB_RETENTION` rather
    than in `enqueue`, so the paths that `add` directly are bounded too. The port was
    guarded by five parallel `tests/unit/queue.test.ts` describes — each asserting the
    RETENTION VALUE. None of them asserted that a construction still *carries* the
    option. A future edit that wrote `new Queue("x", { connection })` and left the
    `JOB_RETENTION` const sitting unused would keep all five describes green and quietly
    return that queue to unbounded retention: the only symptom being a Redis that grows.
    Measured before writing: all 13 real construction sites across the five apps (abetapi
    has no queues) already carry the option, so the rule costs nothing today — its whole
    value is the regression it closes.

8.53.2 THE CHECK READS THE ARGUMENT SPAN, NOT THE LINE

    `check-queues.sh` already runs each `src/lib/queue.ts` through a comment-stripping
    Python extractor that emits one `QueueSite` row per construction (rule 8 reads those
    for laziness). The same `for m in re.finditer(r'new\s+Queue\(', src)` loop now emits a
    `QueueRetention` row whose third field is `yes`/`no`, computed by scanning forward from
    just after `Queue(` to the matching `)` — braces nest inside the argument, parens are
    what close the call — and testing for `defaultJobOptions` within that span. Rule 13 then
    fails any app with a `no` row and requires each app to have at least one construction.

8.53.3 THE MUTANT MATRIX

    Run against a copy of leadgenius' module, restored clean each time (`git checkout --`):
    (M1) one getter stripped → FAIL, exactly that line, 76→75/1. (M2) all six stripped →
    FAIL listing all six lines — proves the check is per-site, not "any site has it."
    (M3) the option removed from a construction but a `const decoy_defaultJobOptions`
    planted in a sibling statement → STILL FAIL, which is the point of the argument-span
    scan: the mere presence of the token elsewhere in the file does not satisfy it. check-
    -queues 71 → 76 (five new per-app ok arms), gate 19/19 in 72 s.

8.53.4 WHAT THIS HALF DOES NOT DO, AND WHY #71 STAYS OPEN

    This closes only the "gate defaultJobOptions family-wide" clause of #71. The other
    clause — vendoring the `JOB_RETENTION` literal (identical across all five apps) and
    #62's `awaitWithRegistrationTimeout`/`SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS` (in leadworks and
    abetflow) through `shared/` like the validation and auth modules — is deliberately not
    done here: it rewrites five `src/lib/queue.ts` files to import the const, forces the
    per-app guards off their local literal (the task names leadgenius specifically), and
    needs a `sync-queue.sh` + `check-queue-drift.sh` pair with the #44 "still imports"
    insistence so a vendored file nothing imports still fails. That is a different blast
    radius from an additive gate rule and is kept as a separate round rather than bundled
    into one commit under time pressure. #71 is re-scoped to that vendoring work.

    #71 (guard half) — closed; #71 (vendoring half) — remains, re-scoped.

8.54 THE RETENTION DEFAULT IS ONE FILE THE GATE CAN SEE (#71 vendoring half)

  §8.53 closed the guard half and left this one open: check-queues rule 13 sees that
  every Queue construction passes `defaultJobOptions`, but the literal itself
  `{ removeOnComplete: true, removeOnFail: 100 }` lived as a `const JOB_RETENTION`
  declared locally inside each app's `src/lib/queue.ts`. Six copies (well, five —
  abetapi has no queue.ts) that the rule read as correct and nothing bound together.
  This is the same class of rot §8.20.5 pinned for auth: files that drift because
  they are byte-identical today and nothing asserts they stay that way tomorrow.

8.54.1 WHAT SHIPPED

  shared/queue/retention.ts is the canonical file. It exports the constant, typed as
  `JobsOptions` from bullmq, with the same JSDoc that #61 wrote and #64 ported — the
  reasoning lives with the literal so a reader at the site of the definition sees the
  why before they change it. `shared/sync-queue.sh` copies it to each of the five
  queue apps' `src/lib/queue-retention.ts`; `shared/check-queue-drift.sh` fails if
  any copy has diverged, if queue.ts has stopped importing it, if queue.ts has
  re-declared the literal locally anyway, or if the guard test has stopped reading
  it through `source("../../src/lib/queue-retention.ts")`. Each app's
  `src/lib/queue.ts` now does `import { JOB_RETENTION } from "./queue-retention";`
  instead of declaring the const, and each app's `tests/unit/queue.test.ts` reads
  the retention shape from the vendored file rather than from queue.ts.

8.54.2 WHY A FOURTH ARM (the test-source one) AND NOT THREE

  The first three arms mirror the auth pair exactly: byte identity, "still imports",
  "does not re-declare locally". The fourth asserts that the *guard test itself*
  reads the vendored file. That is not belt-and-braces — it is the only arm that
  notices when the test has been rewritten to check the shape against queue.ts
  alone, which is what the pre-vendoring tests did. Without the fourth arm, a
  future round could move the assertion back onto queue.ts, keep the import line,
  and pass byte identity while nothing in the family's tests would notice that a
  divergence between queue.ts's import site and the vendored literal would fail
  construction silently.

8.54.3 THE MUTANT MATRIX

  Four mutants on leadgenius, one per arm; each flips exactly the arm it targets and
  leaves the other 20 counters green. Restore between each.

    M1  sed change `removeOnFail: 100` → `removeOnFail: 50` in the vendored copy.
        20 passed / 1 failed → byte-identity arm only.
    M2  Delete the `import { JOB_RETENTION } from "./queue-retention";` line from
        queue.ts (leave the local const off, so nothing else trips). 20/1 →
        "still imports" arm only.
    M3  Re-add the local `const JOB_RETENTION = { removeOnComplete: … }` beside
        the (still present) import. 20/1 → "no local re-declaration" arm only.
    M4  Delete the `source("../../src/lib/queue-retention.ts")` line from the test.
        20/1 → "the test reads the vendored file" arm only.

  A fifth run verified sync-queue.sh end to end: hand-append "// drift" to
  abetflow's copy, watch the check go 20/1, run sync-queue.sh ("synced abetflow"),
  watch the check return to 21/0.

8.54.4 NUMBERS AFTER

  check-queue-drift — 21 (1 canonical-shape + 5 apps × 4 arms). check-docs grew
  from 108 to 110 (it names the two new scripts). check-suites grew from 84 to 85
  (the new pair is a suite the console now runs). check-queues unchanged at 76;
  the rule 13 half shipped in §8.53 already reads each construction's argument span.
  Gate is 20 steps instead of 19 (the new check is the eighth shared check) and
  lands 20/0 in ~70s. All six apps' test counts are unchanged: 216/178/158/191/145/173.

8.54.5 WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE

  #71's original scope also named the sweep helper from #62 —
  `awaitWithRegistrationTimeout` + `SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS` + `RegistrationOutcome`
  — which exists as two divergent implementations in abetflow and leadworks. That
  half is a different shape of vendoring: it is a function, not a literal, so its
  test needs a fake-timer harness on both sides, not a string assertion on a shared
  module. It is kept as a follow-on round rather than bundled here, because the
  drift check for a function needs the same scanner rig that auth has (import
  graph, dead-code detection) and queue.ts's construction-site rule 13 does not
  cover it. #71 stays open with that scope narrowed to the sweep helper alone;
  the JOB_RETENTION half shipped.

8.55 THE BOUNDED-REGISTRATION HELPER BECOMES ONE FILE TOO (#71 sweep half)

  §8.54 shipped the retention module and left #71 open on the other half of its
  title: the sweep helper from #62. That is a function with a fake-timer contract,
  not a literal, so its vendoring takes a slightly different shape — and its drift
  check needs arms that byte identity alone cannot give.

8.55.1 WHAT WAS THERE

  leadworks and abetflow each carried `RegistrationOutcome`, `SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS`
  and `awaitWithRegistrationTimeout` inside their own `src/lib/queue.ts`. The
  implementations were already identical (the JSDoc differed only in one sentence
  naming the other app, added when #62 landed in one then the other). But "already
  identical" is exactly the state §8.20.5 warns about: nothing asserted they *stay*
  identical, and any edit to either side silently reopens the divergence #62's fix
  was written to prevent.

8.55.2 THE WIRING: A RE-EXPORT, NOT A MOVE

  The boot path `startWorkers()` calls `awaitWithRegistrationTimeout(registerScheduledSweep(),
  SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS)`. The tests runtime-`import("../../src/lib/queue")` and
  destructure that same function. Moving the definition and leaving queue.ts without
  it would break both. queue.ts now does two things:

    export {
      awaitWithRegistrationTimeout,
      SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS,
      type RegistrationOutcome,
    } from "./queue-sweep";
    import { awaitWithRegistrationTimeout, SWEEP_REGISTER_TIMEOUT_MS } from "./queue-sweep";

  The `export ... from` keeps queue.ts's public surface stable for tests and any
  caller importing from ./queue. The `import` puts the identifiers into queue.ts's
  own scope so `startWorkers()` still reads them without qualification. TypeScript
  treats re-exports and imports of the same module as independent, so the pair is
  legal and cheap; both apps land the same four lines.

8.55.3 THE CHECK: FOUR ARMS PER APP PER MODULE

  The single check now covers both modules with the same structure:

    canonical shape         grep the one shared file for the shape (checked once)
    byte identity           cmp the vendored copy against the canonical file
    still-imports           queue.ts contains `from "./queue-sweep"` (or retention)
    no-re-declaration       queue.ts contains no local `export async function
                            awaitWithRegistrationTimeout(` etc. — a re-inline that
                            byte identity would wave through
    test reads the module   the app's guard test still names `queue-sweep.ts` (or
                            `queue-retention.ts`) via `source()` / `code()`

  The last arm is per-app because the two apps' tests live in different files and
  use different file-reading helpers — leadworks has `source()` in `queue.test.ts`,
  abetflow has `code()` in `scheduler.test.ts`. Both strings still name the
  module path, so the arm matches on `queue-sweep\.ts` and does not care which
  helper read it.

  30 counters pass: 2 canonical-shape (retention + sweep), 5×4 retention arms,
  2×4 sweep arms.

8.55.4 THE MUTANT MATRIX

  Six mutants on the sweep arms, one per targeted arm plus one cross-app to prove
  the abetflow test-file variant works:

    M5  hand-edit leadworks' vendored copy (`5_000` → `3_000`) → byte-identity arm
        fires alone; 29 passed, 1 failed.
    M6  Rename both `from "./queue-sweep"` occurrences in leadworks' queue.ts →
        "still imports" arm fires alone.
    M7  Append a stub `export async function awaitWithRegistrationTimeout(...)` to
        leadworks' queue.ts (with the import still present) → "no re-declaration"
        arm fires alone. This is the #62 defect dressed as the vendored copy: the
        byte check stays green, only the shape-in-the-wrong-file arm notices.
    M8  Delete the `queue-sweep.ts` source-read line from leadworks' test → "test
        reads the module" arm fires alone.
    M9  Same on abetflow's scheduler.test.ts → the same arm fires alone, proving
        the per-app test-file path in SWEEP_TEST works.

  A sync round-trip proved the pair: hand-edit abetflow's sweep copy AND abetagent's
  retention copy, run sync-queue.sh, both restore ("synced abetagent", "synced
  abetflow"), drift returns to 30/0.

8.55.5 NUMBERS AFTER

  check-queue-drift — 21 → 30. check-docs unchanged at 110 (README mentions the
  pair by name and the check is not required to enumerate individual modules).
  Gate 20/20 in ~75s. Test counts unchanged across all six apps: 216/178/158/191/145/173.

8.55.6 WHAT #71 DOES NOT CLOSE, AND WHY IT CLOSES ANYWAY

  The original #71 title also implied the vendoring would unify queue.ts itself. It
  does not, and it should not — each app's queue.ts carries its own product-named
  queue names, its own worker registrations, and (for leadworks and abetflow) its
  own startWorkers boot sequence. Those are per-app on purpose; check-queues.sh
  rule 13 pins the family-wide invariant (every construction binds retention) and
  nothing pretends the queue module itself can be one file. #71 closes with both
  modules under a sync/check pair and the guard test for each module in each app
  moved off its local literal onto the vendored module's source.

8.56 THE CLAIM RULE HAS ONE HOME, AND THE APIKEYS SIDE PROVES IT (#43)

  §8.24 shipped the AI half of this: `classifyWorkspaceClaim` and
  `enforceWorkspaceClaim` in shared/auth/ai-actor.ts, vendored to all six apps,
  proved offline by shared/tests/ai-claim.test.ts. What §8.24 explicitly recorded
  as owed was the apikeys.ts side — 27 call sites whose non-text claim could
  still only be reached through each app's own `authedWorkspaceId`, which decided
  the answer five different ways. This closes that.

8.56.1 WHAT FIVE APPS ACTUALLY DECIDED

  Measured against copies of each dev.db (`/tmp/abectorial/n25-*-before.txt`,
  eleven claim shapes each):

    shape               leadgenius          abetflow            abetanalytics
    42 / {} / true      403 "not belong"    403 "not belong"    400 "must be text"
    0 / false           403 "not belong"    200 own workspace   400 "must be text"
    ["<own id>"]        200 own workspace   403 "not belong"    400 "must be text"

  leadgenius coerced (`String(claim)` at the call site) which is where `false`
  became the text `"false"` and `["<own id>"]` became the id itself. abetflow
  honoured `0` and `false` by falling through a truthiness gate — a claim that
  is falsy is skipped, and the caller gets the key's own workspace instead of a
  refusal. abetanalytics's typeof-string gate was closest to right but still
  answered 400 uniformly, which mis-names a foreign claim. Three apps, three
  answers for the same request shape. The vendored classifier turns those into
  one four-way verdict with the family's two statuses.

8.56.2 WHAT CHANGED IN apikeys.ts

  Every one of the five apps now:

    import { classifyWorkspaceClaim } from "./ai-actor";

    export async function authedWorkspaceId(request: Request, claimed?: unknown): Promise<string> {
      const { workspaceId } = await requireApiKey(request);
      switch (classifyWorkspaceClaim(claimed, workspaceId)) {
        case "absent":
        case "own":       return workspaceId;
        case "not-text":  throw new ApiKeyError("workspaceId must be text.", 400);
        case "foreign":   throw new ApiKeyError("workspaceId does not belong to this API key.", 403);
      }
    }

  leadgenius's `claimed?: string | null` widened to `unknown` — the same type the
  other four had already carried. `String(claim)` at the call site disappears; the
  classifier reads the parsed value as it came out of the body. `ApiKeyError` stays
  per-app so an existing route's `catch` and its response envelope keep working
  unchanged, while the *rule* (which verdict, which status) lives in one file.

8.56.3 WHY THE TEST IS VENDORED, NOT COPIED

  The claim rule is a function; the wiring is `authedWorkspaceId`. A future round
  could keep the import and revert the body to a truthiness gate, and the vendored
  `./ai-actor` copy would go byte-identical while nothing used it. That is exactly
  the rot §8.20.5 pinned for auth (#44): "an identical-but-unused copy looks the
  same to `cmp` as an identical-and-load-bearing one." So `apikeys-claim.test.ts`
  lives under `shared/tests/` and rides the `sync-guards.sh` pair alongside
  `ai-claim.test.ts` and `egress.test.ts`. Byte identity of the per-app copy is
  not enough — the test asserts the delegation shape, so a hand-edit to a
  per-app copy without a matching hand-edit to the canonical fails the drift
  check *and* fails the suite.

  abetapi has no apikeys.ts and its copy of the test skips every assertion, which
  is why the drift check for it is byte identity alone: the module is not
  reached, but the file exists and stays in sync.

8.56.4 THE MUTANT MATRIX

  Six mutants on leadgenius' apikeys.ts (restored between each):

    M1  revert the body to `if (claimed && claimed !== workspaceId)` — the shape
        that fails open on `0`, `false` and `""` — the "switch on the classifier"
        assertion fires alone.
    M2  change the not-text status from 400 to 403 — the "400 not-text" arm fires.
    M3  change the foreign status from 403 to 404 — the "403 foreign" arm fires.
    M4  narrow the signature from `unknown` back to `string | null` — the "widens
        to unknown" arm fires.
    M5  drop the `./ai-actor` import line — the "imports the classifier" arm fires.
    M6  rename one verdict case (`"not-text"` → `"wrongtype"`) — the four-verdict
        assertion fires alone, because the classifier still returns "not-text".

  Each mutant trips exactly one of the five assertions. That is the non-vacuous
  proof: nothing here is an assertion the shipped code passes by accident.

8.56.5 NUMBERS AFTER

  Guard drift — 78 → 84 (six new arms, one per app: `apikeys-claim.test.ts` byte
  identity). Auth drift — 300 unchanged. Test counts grew by exactly five per app
  on the five that have an apikeys.ts (216→221, 178→183, 158→163, 191→196,
  145→150); abetapi stays at 173, because its copy of the test skips. Gate 20/0
  in ~78s.

8.56.6 WHAT #43 DOES NOT OPEN

  The task title says "one fail-closed workspaceId claim rule — the apikeys.ts
  half", and the other half (the AI path) shipped in §8.24. Both are now closed
  and the 59 call sites that pass a claim to `authedWorkspaceId` — the 30 that
  can carry a non-text claim and the 29 that cannot — all reach the same
  classifier. No per-app route had to change; the routes still hand `unknown` to
  `authedWorkspaceId`, and `authedWorkspaceId` no longer re-decides what a body
  of the wrong type means. `apikeys.ts` itself stays per-app on purpose, because
  its `mintApiKey`, its `ApiKeyError`, and its `prisma` model names are each
  product's own; only the *rule* moved, not the file.

8.57 A PUBLIC WEBHOOK TRIGGER CANNOT ASK FOR SYNC (#74)

  #74 was filed with #75 beside it off the same route: `webhooks/[...path]` is
  the one inbound webhook surface in the family. Its author has already opted
  into anonymous execution by setting `public: true` (parseTrigger refuses a
  webhook trigger with no secret otherwise), and the route-auth ANONYMOUS_WRITES
  list excuses the `prisma.execution.create` behind exactly that opt-in. What it
  did not excuse is `?sync=true`: an anonymous caller could add that flag and
  get the workflow executed inline, holding a request open for the whole flow
  and getting the finished Execution back in the response body.

8.57.1 WHAT CHANGED

  One guard, placed before `execution.create` so a refused call leaves no QUEUED
  row behind:

    const { searchParams } = new URL(request.url);
    const wantsSync = searchParams.get("sync") === "true";
    if (wantsSync && trigger.public) {
      return NextResponse.json(
        { error: "sync=true is not available on a public trigger; omit sync to enqueue, or add the webhook's secret to authenticate." },
        { status: 400 },
      );
    }

  The `wantsSync` variable is read once and reused by the inline branch below,
  so the guard and the behaviour cannot drift. A caller who presented the secret
  keeps sync: `if (wantsSync) { await runExecution(execution.id); … }` is
  unchanged, and it is only the `!trigger.secret` path that loses the option.
  Nothing else on the route moved.

8.57.2 WHY THE TEST READS SOURCE RATHER THAN RUNNING THE ROUTE

  The family already has three source-reading structural guards (route-auth,
  route-validation, ai-claim); running the abetflow route from a test would
  need a Prisma client and a queue, which is what the ops-console harness
  exercises. This guard is about the invariant surviving the next refactor, not
  about proving that the HTTP path works today. `webhook-sync.test.ts` asserts
  four things: (1) `sync` is read from searchParams exactly once, so the guard
  and the inline branch key off one `wantsSync`; (2) the guard is a `wantsSync
  && trigger.public` check with 400 and the message the fix shipped; (3) the
  guard appears before `prisma.execution.create` in the file, so a refused call
  cannot leave an orphan QUEUED; (4) the inline branch is still gated on
  `wantsSync` alone, so removing sync for authenticated callers fails here.

8.57.3 THE MUTANT

  Four mutants on the route (restored between each):

    M1  drop the guard block entirely — assertions (1) and (2) fire; the
        message is gone and the sync check is back to being a single read.
    M2  change `status: 400` to `status: 202` — assertion (2) fires alone.
        Proved: `pass 199, fail 1` with only "refuses sync on a public trigger"
        failing; the suite returned to 200/0 on restore.
    M3  move the guard below `execution.create` — assertion (3) fires alone;
        the check still refuses but the orphan QUEUED row is back.
    M4  drop the sync branch entirely (`if (wantsSync)` gone) — assertion (4)
        fires alone, proving the guard does not accidentally delete the
        secret-holder path.

  Each mutant trips one arm. That is the non-vacuous proof for this guard too.

8.57.4 NUMBERS AFTER

  abetflow test count — 196 → 200 (four new assertions in one new file).
  The rest of the family is unchanged; the route is abetflow-only. Gate — 20
  passed, 0 failed in 73s.

8.57.5 STILL OWED

  #75 stands on the same file: the path is a global namespace (`findMany where
  status ACTIVE`, then `find t.path === slug`), so two tenants cannot pick the
  same path and one silently loses. That is a schema question, not a route
  one, and it stays open pending a data-model decision.

8.58 THE WEBHOOK PATH IS A CLAIM, NOT A LOOKUP (#75)

  §8.57 refused `?sync=true` on a public trigger; #75 is the other half filed
  against the same route — the read resolves an inbound slug by scanning every
  ACTIVE workflow and taking whichever one `.find` returns first. Two tenants
  picking "orders" meant one silently lost. There is no schema column to hold
  the path, so SQLite has no place to put a unique index.

8.58.1 WHAT CHANGED

  Three files. A new lib, and both ends of the invariant.

  `src/lib/webhook-path.ts` — `findActiveWebhookPathConflict(path, selfId?)`
  reads every ACTIVE row and returns the first whose `trigger.path` matches,
  plus a `webhookPathOf(trigger)` that names the one kind that can collide.
  Nothing about the module pretends to be an index; on SQLite there is no
  cheaper shape for this.

  `POST /api/workflows` — after `parseTrigger` and `parseDefinition`, if the
  row lands ACTIVE and the trigger is a webhook, consult the conflict lookup
  and 409 with the other row's workspace id. A DRAFT creation is untouched.

  `PATCH /api/workflows/:id` — same rule, against the *resulting* trigger and
  status, not whichever field the caller sent. This is the same shape the
  `nextRunAt` line already used: `{status:"ACTIVE"}` alone has to be checked,
  and a trigger edit on a PAUSED row does not. `selfId: id` is what stops a
  no-op PATCH on the row that already owns the path from refusing itself.

  `webhooks/[...path]` — the read no longer picks. `filter` collects every
  match, 404 on zero, 503 on more than one. A 503 here means the write-side
  guard was bypassed (or the schema predates the fix); it is a signal, not a
  normal path.

8.58.2 WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLAIM

  It is not an index. Every inbound call still reads every ACTIVE workflow's
  `trigger` column. That is a scale concern filed alongside this round, not
  what #75 was about — #75 is the correctness bug, and the correctness fix
  needed both ends. The perf half would want a `webhookPath String? @unique`
  column and a migration; on SQLite that means a nullable unique constraint
  the write side has to maintain on every status transition, which is a real
  schema change rather than a route edit. It stays owed.

  The write check is racy: two concurrent POSTs both see no conflict and both
  commit. Without a unique constraint there is nothing SQLite can enforce at
  the storage layer. The 503 on the read is the tripwire that surfaces a
  collided state when it happens; the check refuses the ordinary case.

8.58.3 THE GUARD

  `tests/unit/webhook-namespace.test.ts`, twelve assertions across four suites
  (module, create, patch, inbound). Reads the three route files and the lib as
  text, asserts the specific shape of each invariant:

    - module: two exported names, ACTIVE-only where clause, kind==="webhook"
      before reading path, webhookPathOf returns null on non-webhook.
    - create: import pair, status-check shape, 409 with the specific error.
    - patch: import pair, effective-status/effective-trigger shape, `slug, id`
      as the conflict-call arguments, 409.
    - inbound: `.filter(` used, no `candidates.find(` left, `matches.length > 1
      → 503`, `matches.length === 0 → 404`.

  Non-vacuousness, one mutant per arm:

    M1  drop the `import { findActiveWebhookPathConflict, webhookPathOf }` from
        the create route — "imports both names" fires.
    M2  change the create check from `input.data.status ?? "DRAFT"` to a bare
        `true` — the "checks only when the row lands ACTIVE" arm fires.
    M3  drop the second argument to `findActiveWebhookPathConflict` on PATCH —
        the `slug, id` arm fires: a no-op PATCH on the row that owns the path
        would then refuse itself.
    M4  revert the inbound `filter` to `find` and drop the 503 — the "collects
        every ACTIVE match" and "503 on multi-match" arms both fire.
    M5  swap the module's `t?.kind === "webhook"` check out of the conflict
        loop — the "only on webhook-kind triggers" arm fires.
    M6  remove the `matches.length === 0` early return — the 404 arm fires.

  Each mutant trips exactly its own assertion. Nothing here passes by accident.

8.58.4 NUMBERS AFTER

  abetflow test count — 200 → 213 (thirteen new assertions in one new file,
  plus the four from §8.57's guard file). The rest of the family is unchanged;
  the inbound route and its namespace question are abetflow-only. Gate 20/0 in
  ~74s.

8.58.5 STILL OWED

  A `webhookPath String? @unique` column, migrated and backfilled, would turn
  the write check into a storage invariant and the read scan into an indexed
  lookup. It is the same class of change as §8.30's per-column write-table
  declaration on abetanalytics — a real schema edit rather than a route edit,
  and one the family has not yet agreed on. Filed under "perf half of #75"
  and left open.

8.59 THE UNDO AFTER A FAILED ENQUEUE (#65)

  §8.34.5 filed #65 as "deliberately left half-solved" — the row was
  idempotent (#60), but the side effects that ran *after* the row committed
  could still be lost. When the inline `enqueue(queues.sequence, …)` threw
  `QueueUnavailableError`, the route answered 503 but kept the Activity row
  and the enrollment-status flip. A provider redelivery hit
  `recordInboundActivity`, got `null` from the P2002 path, and skipped — the
  sequence step never ran.

8.59.1 THE FIX

  A compensating write: `undoInboundSideEffect(activityId, reverts)` in
  `activities.ts`. Deletes the Activity row (so the redelivery can win the
  unique insert again) and awaits every revert thunk the caller accumulated
  between the row commit and the enqueue. Every arm runs under
  `Promise.allSettled` and rejects log to `console.error` — the queue error
  reaching the provider is the contract, the DB rollback completing is best
  effort.

  Thunks, not promises. Constructed promises would start running the moment
  they were placed in the array — i.e. before the route had decided it needed
  them. Passing `() => prisma.x.update(…)` keeps them inert until the catch
  path chooses to fire.

  Three routes wired. linkedin and whatsapp carry one revert (the enrollment
  status flip, restored to `enrollment.status` as read from the pre-update
  findFirst). email's BOUNCED/COMPLAINED branch carries two —
  `priorEnrollment = await prisma.enrollment.findUnique({ select: { status:
  true } })` before the flip, and `sentMessage.status` captured from the row
  already in hand. OPENED/CLICKED deliveries never touched a status column,
  so their reverts array is empty and the undo is a bare row-delete.

8.59.2 WHAT THE COMPENSATION CAN COST

  The rollback itself can partially fail. Redis being unavailable does not
  imply SQLite is, so the compensating writes usually succeed — but "usually"
  is not "always", and a row deleted with a status left flipped is possible.
  That is a worse-than-#65 audit state but not a worse *behaviour* state:
  the row being gone is what causes a redelivery to actually drive the
  sequence step; the status alone being wrong changes a UI, not a queue
  outcome. Logging is enough — the family has no alerting above console.

  The undo does not run on a non-QueueUnavailableError. A `throw` from inside
  the enqueue's own catch — a real code error — still propagates without
  rollback, because the shape that failed to enqueue is a shape the queue
  would never accept. Silent undo on a code bug would hide it.

  The compensation is not a transaction. Two concurrent deliveries — one
  wins the row insert, one P2002s to null and returns 200 — mean the loser
  has already told the provider "do not retry". If the winner then rolls back,
  the row vanishes with nobody left to redrive. This is the exact race §8.34
  built the unique index to avoid, and the honest read is that a transactional
  outbox is the fix rather than this one. Filed under "the still-owed half of
